\displaystyle\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\omega,0})=\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon})-\nu_{\sigma^{N}\omega,0}(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}(\varphi_{\omega,0})),

and thus using (
4.7
) and (
(C4)
),
for each
\omega\in\Omega
and each
N\in\mathbb{N}
we can write

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\lvert 1-\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\lvert 1-\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon})\right\rvert+\|Q_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\|_{\infty,\sigma^{N}\omega}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varphi_{0}}(\omega)\alpha_{\omega}(N)\|\varphi_{\omega,0}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{\omega}}.

As this holds for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
and as the right-hand side of the previous equation goes to zero as
N\to\infty
, we must in fact have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left\lvert 1-\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right\rvert=0,

which yields the first claim.

Now, for the second claim, using (
3.6
), we note that (
(C7)
) implies

\displaystyle\Delta_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{-1}\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}\left((\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon})(Q_{\sigma^{-N}\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}\varphi_{\sigma^{-N}\omega})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu_{\omega,0}\left(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon}}\cdot Q_{\sigma^{-N}\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-N}\omega,0})\right)}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}\|Q_{\sigma^{-N}\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-N}\omega,0})\|_{\infty,\omega}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}(\omega)\|Q_{\sigma^{-N}\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-N}\omega,0})\|_{\infty,\omega}.

Thus, letting
\varepsilon\to 0
first and then
N\to\infty
, the second claim follows from (
(C4)
).
∎

Now recall from (
2.20
) that if
\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})>0
for each
\varepsilon>0
, then
\theta_{\omega,0}
is given by

\displaystyle\theta_{\omega,0}:=1-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\lambda_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{k+1})^{-1}q_{\omega,0}^{(k)}=1-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{q}_{\omega,0}^{(k)}.

In light of Lemma
4.5
, we see that (
(C1)
)-(
(C8)
) imply (
(P1)
)-(
(P9)
), and thus we have the following Theorem and first main result of this section.

Theorem 4.6
.

Suppose that (
(C1)
)-(
(C7)
) hold for a random open system
(\Omega,\allowbreak m,\allowbreak\sigma,\allowbreak\mathcal{J}_{0},\allowbreak T,\allowbreak\mathcal{B},\allowbreak\mathcal{L}_{0},\allowbreak\nu_{0},\allowbreak\varphi_{0},\allowbreak H_{\varepsilon})
.
For
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
if there is some
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})=0
for
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
then

\displaystyle\lambda_{\omega,0}=\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon},

or if (
(C8)
) holds, then

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}=\theta_{\omega,0}.

Furthermore, the map
\Omega_{+}\ni\omega\mapsto\theta_{\omega,0}
is measurable.

Proof.

All statements follow directly from Theorem
2.2
, except measurability of
\theta_{\omega,0}
, which follows from its construction as a limit of measurable objects.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

As Theorem
2.2
does not require any measurability, one could restate
Theorem
4.6
to hold for sequential open systems satisfying the sequential versions of hypotheses (
(C1)
)-(
(C8)
).

The following lemma will be useful for bounding
\theta_{\omega,0}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Suppose
m(\Omega\backslash\Omega_{+})=0
.
Then for each
\varepsilon>0
and
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\lambda_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{k+1})^{-1}q_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(k)}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}=1.

Proof.

Recall from Section
3.1
that the hole
H_{\varepsilon}\subseteq\mathcal{J}_{0}
is given by

H_{\varepsilon}:=\bigcup_{\omega\in\Omega}\left\{\omega\right\}\times H_{\omega,\varepsilon},

and the skew map
T:\mathcal{J}_{0}\to\mathcal{J}_{0}
is given by

T(\omega,x)=(\sigma\omega,T_{\omega}(x)).

Let
\tau_{H_{\varepsilon}}(\omega,x)
denote the first return time of the point
(\omega,x)\in H_{\varepsilon}
into
H_{\varepsilon}.
For each
k\geq 0
let
B_{k}:=\left\{(\omega,x)\in H_{\varepsilon}:\tau_{H_{\varepsilon}}(\omega,x)=k+1\right\}
be the set of points
(\omega,x)
that remain outside of the hole
H_{\varepsilon}
for exactly
k
iterates, and for each
\omega\in\Omega
we set
B_{k,\omega}:=\pi_{2}(B_{k}\cap\left\{\omega\right\}\times\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0})
. Then
B_{k,\omega}
is precisely the set

B_{k,\omega}=\left\{x\in H_{\omega,\varepsilon}:T_{\omega}^{k+1}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{k+1}\omega,\varepsilon}\text{ and }T_{\omega}^{j}(x)\not\in H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon}\text{ for all }1\leq j\leq k\right\}.

For each
k\geq 0
we can disintegrate
\mu_{0}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\left(B_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{\omega,0}(B_{k,\omega})\ dm(\omega),

where the last equality follows from the
\sigma
-invariance
m
.
In particular, for fixed
\omega\in\Omega_{+}
and each
k\geq 0
we have

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mu_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}(B_{k,\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega})=\mu_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}\left(T_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega}^{-(k+1)}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\cap\left(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k}T_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega}^{-(k+1)+j}(H_{\sigma^{-j}\omega,\varepsilon}^{c})\right)\cap H_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon}\right).

Recall from (
3.8
) that
\hat{q}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(k)}
is given by

\displaystyle\hat{q}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(k)}:=\frac{\mu_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}\left(T_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega}^{-(k+1)}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\cap\left(\bigcap_{j=1}^{k}T_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega}^{-(k+1)+j}(H_{\sigma^{-j}\omega,\varepsilon}^{c})\right)\cap H_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon}\right)}{\mu_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}\left(T_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega}^{-(k+1)}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\right)},

which is well defined since
\omega\in\Omega_{+}
by assumption. Note that since
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}(B_{k,\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega})\leq\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})
we must have that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\leq 1
.
Now, as the measure
\mu_{0}
is
T
-invariant, the right-hand side of (
4.9
) is equal to

\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})
, and therefore

(4.10)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\ \mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\ dm(\omega)=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{\omega,0}(B_{k,\omega})\ dm(\omega)=\mu_{0}(B_{k}).

By the Poincaré recurrence theorem we have

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu_{0}(B_{k})=\mu_{0}(H_{\varepsilon})=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\,dm(\omega).

By interchanging the sum with the integral above (possible by Tonelli’s Theorem) and using (
4.10
), we have

\int_{\Omega}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\ \mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\right)\ dm(\omega)=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\ dm(\omega),

which implies

\int_{\Omega}\left(\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}-1\right)\right)dm(\omega)=0.

Since we already have that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\leq 1
for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
, we must in fact have
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\hat{q}^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}=1
, which completes the proof.
∎

By definition, we have that
\hat{q}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(k)}\in[0,1]
and thus (
(C8)
) implies that
\hat{q}_{\omega,0}^{(k)}\in[0,1]
for each
k\geq 0
as well. In light of the previous lemma, dominated convergence implies that

(4.11)

\displaystyle\theta_{\omega,0}\in[0,1].

4.1.
Escape Rate Asymptotics

If we have some additional
\omega
-control on the size of the holes we obtain the following corollary of Theorem
4.6
, which provides a formula for the escape rate asymptotics for small random holes.
The scaling of the holes (
4.13
) takes a similar form to the scaling we will shortly use in the next section for our quenched extreme value theory.

Corollary 4.9
.

Suppose that (
(C1)
)–(
(C8)
) hold for a random open system
(\Omega,\allowbreak m,\allowbreak\sigma,\allowbreak\mathcal{J}_{0},\allowbreak T,\allowbreak\mathcal{B},\allowbreak\mathcal{L}_{0},\allowbreak\nu_{0},\allowbreak\varphi_{0},\allowbreak H_{\varepsilon})
and there exists
A\in L^{1}(m)
such that for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
and all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small we have

(4.12)

\displaystyle\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}\leq A(\omega).

Further suppose that there is some
\kappa(\varepsilon)
with
\kappa(\varepsilon)\to\infty
as
\varepsilon\to 0
,
t\in L^{\infty}(m)
with
t_{\omega}>0
, and
\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(m)
with
\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
such that

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})=\frac{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\kappa(\varepsilon)}

and
|\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}|<Ct_{\omega}
for all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small for some
C>0
.
Then for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{R_{\varepsilon}(\mu_{\omega,0})}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}=\frac{\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0}t_{\omega}\,dm(\omega)}{t_{\omega}}.

In particular, if
t_{\omega}
is constant
m
-a.e. then

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{R_{\varepsilon}(\mu_{\omega,0})}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}=\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0}\,dm(\omega).

Proof.

First, we note that (
4.13
) implies that
\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})>0
for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
and all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, i.e.
m(\Omega\backslash\Omega_{+})=0
.
Using Theorem
4.6
we have that

(4.14)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}=\theta_{\omega,0}

since

\displaystyle\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}\cdot\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}\longrightarrow\frac{1}{\lambda_{\omega,0}}\cdot\lambda_{\omega,0}\theta_{\omega,0}=\theta_{\omega,0}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
In light of (
4.3
) and (
4.4
), we use (
4.13
) to get that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{R_{\varepsilon}(\mu_{\omega,0})}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{\log\lambda_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}(H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon})}\cdot\frac{\mu_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}(H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon})}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{\log\lambda_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}(H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon})}\cdot\frac{t_{\sigma^{j}\omega}+\xi_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon}}{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}}\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{\log\lambda_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}(H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon})}\cdot(t_{\sigma^{j}\omega}+\xi_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}\cdot(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon})\,dm(\omega),

where the last line follows from Birkhoff (which is applicable thanks to (
4.12
) and the fact that
t_{\omega},\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(m)
).
As
\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon}\to 0
by assumption, (
4.12
) allows us to apply Dominated Convergence which, in view of (
4.14
), implies

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{R_{\varepsilon}(\mu_{\omega,0})}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}=\frac{1}{t_{\omega}}\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}\cdot(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,\varepsilon})\,dm(\omega)=\frac{\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0}t_{\omega}\,dm(\omega)}{t_{\omega}},

completing the proof.
∎

In the next section we will present easily checkable assumptions which will imply the hypotheses of Corollary
4.9
.

5.
Quenched extreme value law

5.1.
Gumbel’s law in the quenched regime

Suppose that
h_{\omega}:\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}\to\mathbb{R}
is a continuous function for each
\omega\in\Omega
.
In our quenched random extreme value theory,
h_{\omega}
is an observation function that is allowed to depend on
\omega
.
For each
\omega\in\Omega
let
\overline{z}_{\omega}
be the essential supremum of
h_{\omega}
with respect to
\nu_{\omega,0}
, that is

\displaystyle\overline{z}_{\omega}:=\sup\left\{z\in\mathbb{R}\;:\nu_{\omega,0}(\left\{x\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}:h_{\omega}(x)\geq z\right\})>0\right\}.

Similarly we define
\underline{z}_{\omega}
to be the essential infimum of
h_{\omega}
with respect to
\nu_{\omega,0}
.
Suppose
\underline{z}_{\omega}<\overline{z}_{\omega}
for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
,
and for each
z\in[\underline{z}_{\omega},\overline{z}_{\omega}]
we define the set

\displaystyle V_{\omega,z_{\omega}}:=\left\{x\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}:h_{\omega}(x)-z_{\omega}>0\right\},

which represents points
x
in our phase space where the observation
h_{\omega}
exceeds a random threshold
z_{\omega}
at base configuration
\omega
.
Our theory allows for random thresholds
z_{\omega}
so that we may consider both “anomalous” and “absolute” exceedances.
For example in a real-world application,
h_{\omega}(x)
may represent the surface ocean temperature at a spatial location
x
for an ocean system configuration
\omega
.
Random temperature exceedances above
z_{\omega}
allow one to describe extreme value statistics for temperature anomalies, e.g. those above a climatological seasonal mean (on average the surface ocean is warmer in summer and colder in winter).
On the other hand, non-random absolute temperature exceedances above
z
are more relevant for marine life.
We suppose that

\displaystyle\nu_{\omega,0}(V_{\omega,\overline{z}_{\omega}})=0.

As is standard in extreme value theory, to develop an exponential law we will consider an increasing sequence of thresholds
z_{\omega,0}<z_{\omega,1}<\cdots
.
For each
N\geq 0
we take
z_{\omega,N}\in[\underline{z}_{\omega},\overline{z}_{\omega}]
and the choice will be made explicit in a moment.
For each
k,N\in\mathbb{N}
define
G_{\omega,N}^{(k)}:\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle G_{\omega,N}^{(k)}(x):=h_{\sigma^{N}\omega}(T_{\omega}^{N}(x))-z_{\sigma^{N}\omega,k}.

If
G_{\omega,N}^{(k)}>0
then
h_{\sigma^{N}\omega}(T_{\omega}^{N}(x))>z_{\sigma^{N}\omega,k}
.
Our extreme value law concerns the large
N
limit of the likelihood of continued threshold non-exceedances:

(5.1)

\nu_{\omega,0}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}:\max\left(G_{\omega,0}^{(N)}(x),\dots,G_{\omega,N-1}^{(N)}(x)\right)\leq 0\right\}\right).

We may easily transform (
5.1
) into the language of random open systems:
one immediately has that
G_{\omega,N}^{(k)}>0
is equivalent to
T_{\omega}^{N}(x)\in V_{\sigma^{N}\omega,z_{\sigma^{N}\omega,k}}
.
Therefore, for each
N\geq 0
we have

(5.2)

(\ref{evtexpression})=\nu_{\omega,0}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}:T_{\omega}^{j}(x)\notin V_{\sigma^{j}\omega,z_{\sigma^{j}\omega,N}}\text{ for }j=0,\dots,N-1\right\}\right)\\
=\nu_{\omega,0}\left(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}\right),

where to obtain the second equality we
identify the sets
V_{\omega,z_{\omega,N}}
with holes
H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\subset\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}
for each
N\in\mathbb{N}

1
1

1

In Section
5
we consider a decreasing sequence of holes, whereas in previous sections we considered a decreasing family of holes
H_{\omega,\varepsilon}
parameterised by
\varepsilon>0
. For the sake of notational continuity, in this section, and in the sequel, we denote a decreasing sequence of holes by
H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}
.
Note that
\varepsilon_{N}
here is just a parameter and should not be thought of as a real number, but instead as an index and that
H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}
serves as an alternative notation to
H_{\omega,N}
. Furthermore, note that the measure of the hole depends on the fiber
\omega
with
\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})\to 0
as
N\to\infty
.
.
Now using (
4.1
) and (
5.2
) we may convert (
5.1
) into the spectral expression:

(5.3)

(\ref{evtexpression})=\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}\left(\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\mathbbold{1})+\nu_{\sigma^{N}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}(\mathbbold{1})\right)\right).

Similarly, using (
4.2
), we can write

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}:T_{\omega}^{j}(x)\notin V_{\sigma^{j}\omega,z_{\sigma^{j}\omega,N}}\text{ for }j=0,\dots,N-1\right\}\right)=\mu_{\omega,0}\left(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)

(5.4)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}\left(\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})+\nu_{\sigma^{N}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right)\right).

Before we state our main result concerning the
N\to\infty
limit, in addition to (
(C2)
), (
(C3)
), and (
(C8)
),
we make the following uniform adjustments to some of the assumptions in Section
4
as well as an assumption on the choice of the sequence of thresholds, condition (
(S)
).
At the end of this section, we will compare it with the Hüsler condition, which is the usual prescription for non stationary processes, as we anticipated in the Introduction.

(S)

For any fixed random scaling function
t\in L^{\infty}(m)
with
t>0
, we may find sequences of functions
z_{N},\xi_{N}\in L^{\infty}(m)
and a constant
W<\infty
satisfying

\mu_{\omega,0}(\{h_{\omega}(x)-z_{\omega,N}>0\})=(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})/N,\mbox{ for a.e.\ $\omega$ and each $N\geq 1$}

where:

(i)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\xi_{\omega,N}=0
for a.e.
\omega
and

(ii)
|\xi_{\omega,N}|\leq W
for a.e.
\omega
and all
N\geq 1
.

(C1’)

There exists
C_{1}\geq 1
such that for
m
-e.a.
\omega\in\Omega
we have

\displaystyle C_{1}^{-1}\leq\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}\mathbbold{1}\leq C_{1}.

(C4’)

For each
f\in\mathcal{B}
and each
N\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
C_{f}>0
and
\alpha(N)>0
(independent of
\omega
) with
\alpha:=\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\alpha(N)<\infty
such that for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
, all
N\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\geq 0}\|Q_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{N}f_{\omega}\|_{\infty,\sigma^{N}\omega}\leq C_{f}\alpha(N)\|f_{\omega}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{\omega}}.

(C5’)

There exists
C_{2}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\geq 0}\|\varphi_{\omega,\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,\omega}\leq C_{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\varphi_{\omega,0}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{\omega}}\leq C_{2}

for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.

(C7’)

There exists
C_{3}\geq 1
such that for all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small we have

\displaystyle\essinf_{\omega}\inf\varphi_{\omega,0}\geq C_{3}^{-1}>0\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\essinf_{\omega}\inf\varphi_{\omega,\varepsilon}\geq 0.

Remark 5.1
.

Note that (
(S)
) implies that
\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>0
for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
since
t_{\omega}>0
, and in particular we have
m(\Omega\backslash\Omega_{+})=0
.

Remark 5.2
.

Note that since
\lambda_{\omega,0}=\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}\mathbbold{1})
, (
(C1’)
) implies that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{-1}\leq\lambda_{\omega,0}\leq C_{1}

for
m
-a.e.
\omega
.

Remark 5.3
.

Note that conditions (
(S)
), (
(C5’)
), and (
(C7’)
) together imply (
(C6)
), thus Theorem
4.6
applies.
Furthermore, these same conditions along with Remark
5.2
imply that there exists
A\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\log\lambda_{\omega,0}-\log\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})}\leq A

for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
and all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, and thus (
4.12
) and (
4.13
) hold, meaning that Corollary
4.9
applies as well.

The following lemma shows that
\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})
converges to
1
uniformly in
\omega
under our assumptions (
(C1’)
), (
(C4’)
), (
(C5’)
), (
(C7’)
), and (
(S)
).

Lemma 5.4
.

For each
N\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
C_{\varepsilon_{N}}\geq 1
with
C_{\varepsilon_{N}}\to 1
as
N\to\infty
such that

(5.5)

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon_{N}}^{-1}\leq\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\mathbbold{1}),\,\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\leq C_{\varepsilon_{N}}

for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.

Proof.

Note that

(5.6)

\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}\left((\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0})\right)=\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\omega,0}\left(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}\right).

Further, by (
(C7’)
) we have

(5.7)

\nu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})\leq C_{3}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})=\frac{C_{3}(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{N}

for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.
Following the same derivation of (
2.3
), and using (
(C2)
) gives that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}

\displaystyle=\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}\left((\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})(\varphi_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})\right)=\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\omega,0}(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\varphi_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}).

Thus it follows from Remark
4.2
, (
5.8
), (
(C5’)
), and (
5.7
) that

(5.9)

0\leq\lambda_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\leq\frac{C_{2}C_{3}\lambda_{\omega,0}(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{N}.

Using Remark
5.2
and (
5.9
), for all
N
sufficiently large, we see that

(5.10)

\displaystyle 1\leq\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}}{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}=\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\omega,0}(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\varphi_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}\leq\frac{1}{1-C_{2}\nu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}\leq\frac{1}{1-E_{N}},

where

\displaystyle E_{N}:=\frac{C_{2}C_{3}(|t|_{\infty}+W)}{N}\to 0

as
N\to\infty
.

Claim 5.4.1
.

For every
n
,
\varepsilon_{N}
, and
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert 1-\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right\rvert\leq n\left(\frac{1}{1-E_{N}}\right)^{n}\cdot\left(C_{1}E_{N}+\frac{C_{1}C_{2}E_{N}}{1-E_{N}}\right)+C_{2}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha(n).

Proof.

Using (
(C2)
) and a telescoping argument, we can write

\displaystyle\left\lvert 1-\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}(\varphi_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0})-\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}(\varphi_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0})-\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\big(\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\big)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(\varphi_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}(\varphi_{\omega,0})+Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0})-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k+1}(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

(5.11)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\right)\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

(5.12)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert.

First we note that we can estimate (
5.12
) as

(5.13)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert\leq\|Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\|_{\infty,\sigma^{n}\omega}\leq C_{2}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha(n).

Now recall that

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}:=\mathbbold{1}_{\bigcap_{j=0}^{k}T_{\sigma^{-k}\omega}^{-j}(\mathcal{J}_{\sigma^{j-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}.

Using (
(C2)
), we can write (
5.11
) as

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\right)\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n}\omega,0}\left(\left(\lambda_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\right)^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}^{k}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\cdot\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right)\right\rvert

(5.14)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}^{k}}{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\cdot\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert.

Now, since
\nicefrac{{\lambda_{\omega,0}}}{{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}}\geq 1
(by Remark
4.2
) for
m
a.e.
\omega
and
\varepsilon\geq 0
, and thus
\nicefrac{{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}^{k}}}{{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}}}\geq 1
for each
k\geq 1
, we can write

(
5.14
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{n}}{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\cdot\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{1-E_{N}}\right)^{n}\cdot\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\cdot\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert,

Using

\displaystyle\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,0}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}+\lambda_{\omega,0}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\omega,0}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right)+(\lambda_{\omega,0}^{-1}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{-1})\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\omega,0}^{-1}\left(\left(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right)+\lambda_{\omega,0}\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}(\lambda_{\omega,0}^{-1}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{-1})\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\omega,0}^{-1}\left(\left(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right)+(\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}-\lambda_{\omega,0})\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right),

the fact that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\left((f_{\sigma\omega}\circ T_{\omega})\cdot h_{\omega}\right)=f_{\sigma\omega}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(h_{\omega}),

for all
\varepsilon_{N}\geq 0
and
\omega\in\Omega
, and Remark
5.2
, we may estimate the sum in (
5.15
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\cdot\left(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\cdot\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{-1}\left\lvert(\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}-\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\cdot\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\cdot\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0})\right)\right\rvert

(5.16)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)\left(\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\circ T_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\right)\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\right)\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\bigg(\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{-1}\left\lvert 1-\frac{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}}{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\right\rvert

(5.17)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\cdot\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\left(\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\circ T_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\right)\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\right)\right)\right\rvert\bigg).

Using (
5.8
), (
5.9
), and Remark
5.2
we can estimate
(
5.16
) to get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)\left(\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\circ T_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\right)\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\right)\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\left(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\cdot\left(\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\circ T_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\right)\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\right)\right)\right\rvert

(5.18)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\nu_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\left(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\right)\|\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\|_{\infty,\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\leq\frac{C_{2}C_{3}(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})\cdot n}{N}\leq nE_{N}.

Since
\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}(f)=\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}(\hat{X}_{\omega,0,\varepsilon}f)
we can rewrite the second product in the sum in (
5.17
) so that we have

\displaystyle\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\left(\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\circ T_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\right)\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0,\varepsilon_{N}}\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\circ T_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\right)\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\right)\right)

(5.19)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\|\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\mathbbold{1}\|_{\infty,\sigma^{n-k}\omega}\|\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\|_{\infty,\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\leq C_{1}C_{2}.

Inserting (
5.19
) into (
5.17
)
and using (
5.10
) yields

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left\lvert 1-\frac{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}}{\lambda_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\right\rvert\cdot\left\lvert\nu_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,0}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\left(\left(\hat{X}_{\sigma^{n-k}\omega,k,\varepsilon_{N}}\circ T_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega}\right)\cdot\varphi_{\sigma^{n-(k+1)}\omega,0}\right)\right)\right\rvert

(5.20)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{C_{1}C_{2}E_{N}}{1-E_{N}}=n\cdot\frac{C_{1}C_{2}E_{N}}{1-E_{N}}.

Thus, collecting the estimates (
5.16
)-(
5.20
) together with (
5.13
) and inserting into (
5.15
) yields

(5.21)

\displaystyle\left\lvert 1-\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right\rvert\leq\left(\frac{1}{1-E_{N}}\right)^{n}\cdot\left(nE_{N}+\frac{C_{1}C_{2}E_{N}\cdot n}{1-E_{N}}\right)+C_{2}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha(n),

which finishes the proof of the claim.
∎

To finish the proof of Lemma
5.4
, we note that (
5.21
) holds for
m
-a.e.
\omega
, every
N
sufficiently large, and each
n\geq 1
.
Given a
\delta>0
, choose and fix
n
so that
C_{2}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha(n)<\delta/2
.
Because
\lim_{N\to\infty}E_{N}=0
,
we may choose
N
large enough so that first summand in (
5.21
) is also smaller than
\delta/2
.
Thus,
\lim_{N\to\infty}\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})=1
, uniformly in
\omega
.
This proves (
5.5
) for
\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})
; we immediately obtain the other inequality using (
(C5’)
) and (
(C7’)
), and thus the proof of Lemma
5.4
is complete.

∎

We now obtain a formula for the explicit form of Gumbel law for the extreme value distribution.

Theorem 5.5
.

Given a random open system
(\Omega,\allowbreak m,\allowbreak\sigma,\allowbreak\mathcal{J}_{0},\allowbreak T,\allowbreak\mathcal{B},\allowbreak\mathcal{L}_{0},\allowbreak\nu_{0},\allowbreak\varphi_{0},\allowbreak H_{\varepsilon})
satisfying (
(C1’)
), (
(C2)
), (
(C3)
), (
(C4’)
), (
(C5’)
), (
(C7’)
), (
(C8)
), and (
(S)
),
for almost every
\omega\in\Omega
one has

(5.22)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\nu_{\omega,0}\left(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu_{\omega,0}\left(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}=\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\theta_{\omega,0}\,dm(\omega)\right).

Proof.

Step 1: Estimating
\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}/\lambda_{\omega,0}
.

To work towards constructing an estimate for
\lambda^{N}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}/\lambda^{N}_{\omega,0}
, we first estimate
\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}/\lambda_{\omega,0}
.
For brevity, in this step we drop the
N
subscripts on
\varepsilon
.
Following the proof of Theorem
2.2
up to equation (
2.15
), which uses assumptions (
(C2)
) and (
(C3)
), we have

\displaystyle\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}-\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\Delta_{\omega,\varepsilon}}

(5.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{1-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\lambda_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{-1}(\lambda^{k}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon})^{-1}q^{(k)}_{\omega,\varepsilon}}_{=:\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}

(5.24)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Delta^{-1}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{-1}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}(\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}-\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon})\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}((\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon})(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon}^{k})(\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}))}_{=:\theta^{\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}

(5.25)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Delta^{-1}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\underbrace{\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon})(Q^{n}_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0}))}_{=:\theta^{\prime\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}.

By first rearranging to solve for
\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}
we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{\omega,0}-\frac{\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}\Delta_{\omega,\varepsilon}+\theta^{\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}+\theta^{\prime\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}{\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})},

and thus

(5.26)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}}=1-\underbrace{\frac{\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}\Delta_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}}_{=:Y^{(1)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}-\underbrace{\frac{\theta^{\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}}_{Y^{(2)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}-\underbrace{\frac{\theta^{\prime\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}}_{=:Y^{(3)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}}.

Setting
Y_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}:=Y^{(1)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}+Y^{(2)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}+Y^{(3)}_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}
applying Taylor to
\log(1-\cdot)
, we obtain

(5.27)

\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}}=\exp\left(-Y_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}-\frac{Y_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}^{2}}{2(1-y)^{2}}\right),

where
0\leq y\leq Y_{\omega,\varepsilon,n}
.
Setting
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{N}
in (
5.27
) we obtain

(5.28)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{N}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}-\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{2}}{2(1-y)^{2}}\right)

(5.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\left(g^{(1)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)+g^{(2)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)+g^{(3)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)\right)-\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{2}}{2(1-y)^{2}}\right),

where
g^{(j)}_{N,n}(\omega)=NY^{(j)}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}
for
j=1,2,3
and
0\leq y\leq Y_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}
.

Step 2: A non-standard ergodic lemma.

In preparation for estimating the products along orbits contained in
\lambda^{N}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}/\lambda^{N}_{\omega,0}
, we state and prove a non-standard ergodic lemma.

Lemma 5.6
.

For
N\geq 0
, let
g_{N}\in L^{1}(m)
.
Suppose that as
N\to\infty
,
g_{N}\to g

m
-almost everywhere for some
g\in L^{1}(m)
and that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}|g_{N}-g|\,dm=0
.
Then for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
,
\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g_{N}(\sigma^{i}\omega)
exists and equals
\mathbb{E}(g)=\int_{\Omega}g\ dm
.

Proof.

We write

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g_{N}(\sigma^{i}\omega)-\mathbb{E}(g)\right|\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}|g_{N}(\sigma^{i}\omega)-g(\sigma^{i}\omega)|+\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g(\sigma^{i}\omega)-\mathbb{E}(g)\right|.

First, we note that the Birkhoff Ergodic Theorem implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g(\sigma^{i}\omega)-\mathbb{E}(g)\right|=0.

Now, to deal with the remaining term, since
g_{N}\to g
almost everywhere, given
\delta>0
, we let
N_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
be sufficiently large such that

\displaystyle m\left(\Omega_{\delta}:=\left\{\omega\in\Omega:|g(\omega)-g_{N}(\omega)|<\delta\text{ for all }N\geq N_{\delta}\right\}\right)>1-\delta.

Birkhoff applied to
\mathbbold{1}_{\Omega_{\delta}}
then gives that for each
\delta>0
and all
N\geq N_{\delta}
sufficiently large we have that

(5.30)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\#\left\{0\leq k<N:\sigma^{k}\omega\in\Omega_{\delta}\right\}>1-\delta.

Since
\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}|g_{N}-g|\,dm(\omega)=0
, for each
\delta>0
there exists
N_{\delta}^{\prime}>0
such that
\int_{\Omega}|g_{N}-g|\,dm<\delta
for all
N\geq N_{\delta}^{\prime}
.
We apply the Birkhoff Ergodic Theorem to the functions
|g_{N}-g|\cdot\mathbbold{1}_{\Omega_{\delta}}
and
|g_{N}-g|\cdot\mathbbold{1}_{\Omega_{\delta}^{c}}
.
Using (
5.30
) gives that for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
and all
N\geq\max(N_{\delta},N_{\delta}^{\prime})
sufficiently large (so that the Birkhoff error is less than
\delta
, noting that
N
depends on
\omega
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}|g_{N}(\sigma^{i}\omega)-g(\sigma^{i}\omega)|

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}|g_{N}(\sigma^{i}\omega)-g(\sigma^{i}\omega)|\mathbbold{1}_{\Omega_{\delta}}(\sigma^{i}\omega)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}|g_{N}(\sigma^{i}\omega)-g(\sigma^{i}\omega)|\mathbbold{1}_{\Omega_{\delta}^{c}}(\sigma^{i}\omega)

\displaystyle\qquad<\delta+\int_{\Omega_{\delta}^{c}}|g_{N}-g|\,dm+\delta\leq 2\delta+\int_{\Omega}|g_{N}-g|\,dm<3\delta.

As this holds for every
\delta>0
, we must in fact have that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g_{N}(\sigma^{i}\omega)-\mathbb{E}(g)\right|=0

as desired.
∎

Step 3: Estimating
g^{(1)}
.

In this step we construct estimates of
g^{(1)}
that are required to apply Lemma
5.6
.

In preparation for the first use of Lemma
5.6
we recall that

g_{N,n}^{(1)}(\omega):=\frac{N\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}\Delta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}\\
=\frac{N\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}{\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}=\frac{\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})},

where
\lim_{N\to\infty}\xi_{\omega,N}=0
for a.e.
\omega
and
|\xi_{\omega,N}|\leq W
by (
(S)
).
We also set
g_{n}^{(1)}(\omega):=\theta_{\omega,0,n}t_{\omega}
, where

(5.31)

\displaystyle\theta_{\omega,0,n}:=1-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left(\lambda_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{k+1}\right)^{-1}q_{\omega,0}^{(k)}=1-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\hat{q}_{\omega,0}^{(k)}.

By Lemma
4.8
and (
(C8)
) we see that
0\leq\hat{q}_{\omega,0}^{(k)},\hat{q}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{(k)}\leq 1
for each
k
,
N
, and
m
-a.e.
\omega
. Thus, (
(C8)
) and the Dominated Convergence Theorem imply that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\|\hat{q}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{(k)}-\hat{q}_{\omega,0}^{(k)}\|_{1}=0
. From (
5.23
) we have that

(5.32)

\displaystyle\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}:=1-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\lambda_{\sigma^{-(k+1)}\omega,0}^{-1}(\lambda^{k}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})^{-1}q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{(k)}=1-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}^{k}}{\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}}\hat{q}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{(k)},

and thus that
\theta_{\omega,0,N}\in[0,1]
and
\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}\leq 1
for each
n
and
m
-a.e.
\omega
.
Using (
5.10
) and the fact that
\nicefrac{{\lambda_{\omega,0}}}{{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}}\geq 1
(by Remark
4.2
), we have that

\displaystyle\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}\geq 1-\frac{\lambda_{\sigma^{-(n-1)}\omega,0}^{n-1}}{\lambda_{\sigma^{-(n-1)}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n-1}}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\hat{q}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{(k)}\geq 1-\left(1+\frac{C_{1}E_{N}}{C_{1}-E_{N}}\right)^{n}.

For fixed
n
, again we apply Dominated Convergence to get that

(5.33)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\|\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}-\theta_{\omega,0,n}\|_{1}=0.

Using Lemma
5.4
and (
(S)
) we see that
g_{N,n}^{(1)},g_{n}^{(1)}\in L^{1}(m)
for each
N,n
.
Moreover using these same facts

\displaystyle\|g_{N,n}^{(1)}-g_{n}^{(1)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\left(\left|\frac{\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}-\theta_{\omega,0,n}t_{\omega}\right|\right)\ dm.

The integrand goes to zero
\omega
almost surely and it is bounded by
C_{\varepsilon_{N}}(|t|_{\infty}+W)+|t|_{\infty}.
We could therefor apply dominated convergence to prove that the
L^{1}(m)
difference goes to zero.

Referring to the first term in the
Y
-sum in (
5.28
), we may now apply Lemma
5.6
to conclude that

\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{\theta_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}\Delta_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}{\lambda_{\sigma^{i}\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n+i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n+i}\omega,0})}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g_{N,n}^{(1)}(\sigma^{i}\omega)\to\int_{\Omega}g_{n}^{(1)}(\omega)\ dm(\omega)=\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0,n}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega),

as
N\to\infty
for each
n
and
m
-a.e.
\omega
.

Step 4: Estimating
g^{(2)}
.

We now perform a similar analysis to the previous step to control the terms in the sum (
5.28
) corresponding to
\theta^{\prime}
.
Again in preparation for applying Lemma
5.6
, recall that

g_{N,n}^{(2)}(\omega)=\frac{N\theta^{\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}

and set
g_{n}^{(2)}(\omega)\equiv 0
for each
n
.
Using (
5.24
), (
5.6
), (
5.9
), and (
(C7’)
), for sufficiently large
N
we have

\displaystyle\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda^{-1}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}(\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}-\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon})\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}((\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}-\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon})(\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon}^{k})(\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{2}C_{3}\lambda_{\omega,0}}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}^{-1}\left(\lambda_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}(t_{\sigma^{-k}\omega}+\xi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,N})\right)\nu_{\omega,0}\left(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{2}C_{3}\lambda_{\omega,0}}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(t_{\sigma^{-k}\omega}+\xi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,N})\nu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})\|\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}\|_{\infty,\omega}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{2}C_{3}\lambda_{\omega,0}}{N}\cdot\frac{C_{3}(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(t_{\sigma^{-k}\omega}+\xi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,N})\|\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}\|_{\infty,\omega}.

Using Lemma
5.4
, (
(C5’)
), and (
(C4’)
) we note that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{}\mathcal{L}_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}\|_{\infty,\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\nu_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0})\varphi_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}+Q_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{k}\varphi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,0}\|_{\infty,\omega}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|C_{\varepsilon_{N}}C_{2}+C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}\alpha(k)\|_{\infty,\omega}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}C_{\varepsilon_{N}}+C_{2}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha.

Finally, we have for
N
sufficiently large and by (
(S)
)

\displaystyle g_{N,n}^{(2)}(\omega)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{NC_{2}C_{3}\lambda_{\omega,0}(C_{2}C_{\varepsilon_{N}}+C_{2}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha)}{N\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}\cdot\frac{C_{3}(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(t_{\sigma^{-k}\omega}+\xi_{\sigma^{-k}\omega,N}).

Integrability of
g_{N,n}^{(2)}
follows from the the fact that
t\in L^{\infty}(m)
and
|\xi_{\omega,N}|\leq W,
for almost all
\omega.

Moreover by (
(S)
), for each
n
we have that
g_{N,n}^{(2)}\to 0
almost everywhere as
N\to\infty
.
By dominated convergence we have again that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\|g_{N,n}^{(2)}-g_{n}^{(2)}\|_{1}\to 0
for each
n
.

Referring to
Y_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{(2)}
in (
5.26
), we may now apply Lemma
5.6
to conclude that

\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{\theta^{\prime}_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}}{\lambda_{\sigma^{i}\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n+i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n+i}\omega,0})}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g_{N,n}^{(2)}(\sigma^{i}\omega)\to\int_{\Omega}g_{n}^{(2)}(\omega)\ dm(\omega)=0,

as
N\to\infty
for each
n
and a.e.
\omega
.

Step 5: Estimating
g^{(3)}
.

We repeat a similar analysis to control the terms in the sum (
5.28
) corresponding to
\theta^{\prime\prime}
.
Again in preparation for applying Lemma
5.6
, recall that

g_{N,n}^{(3)}(\omega)=\frac{N\theta^{\prime\prime}_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})}.

We begin developing an upper bound for
|g_{N,n}^{(3)}|
.
Using (
5.25
), (
(C2)
), (
(C4’)
), and (
(C7’)
) we have for sufficiently large
N
that

\displaystyle|\theta_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{\prime\prime}|

\displaystyle:=\nu_{\sigma\omega,0}(\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}Q^{n}_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})))

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\omega,0}(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}}Q_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})\|Q_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{n}\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0}\|_{\infty,\omega}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})C_{\varphi_{0}}\|\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{\sigma^{-n}\omega}}\alpha(n)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\omega,0}C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}C_{3}\alpha(n)\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{\omega,0}C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}C_{3}\alpha(n)(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{N}.

Therefore, using Lemma
5.4

|g_{N,n}^{(3)}(\omega)|\leq\frac{N\lambda_{\omega,0}C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}C_{3}\alpha(n)(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})}{N\lambda_{\omega,0}\nu_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{-n}\omega,0}))}\leq C_{\varepsilon_{N}}C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}C_{3}\alpha(n)(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})=:\tilde{g}^{(3)}_{N,n}(\omega).

We set
\tilde{g}^{(3)}_{n}(\omega)=C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}C_{3}\alpha(n)t_{\omega}
.
Integrability of
\tilde{g}_{N,n}^{(3)}
and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{(3)}
follows from (
(S)
) and the fact
t\in L^{\infty}(m)
and
|\xi_{\omega,N}|\leq W,
for almost all
\omega.

Similarly, (recalling that
C_{\varepsilon_{N}}\to 1
as
N\to\infty
by Lemma
5.4
) for each
n
,
\tilde{g}_{N,n}^{(3)}\to\tilde{g}_{n}^{(3)}
almost everywhere as
N\to\infty
.
For the same reasons
by (
(S)
) and dominated convergence we also have that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\|\tilde{g}_{N,n}^{(3)}-\tilde{g}_{n}^{(3)}\|_{1}\to 0
for each
n
.

Referring to
Y_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{(3)}
in (
5.26
), we may now apply Lemma
5.6
to
\tilde{g}_{N,n}^{(3)}
and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{(3)}
to conclude that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}g_{N,n}^{(3)}(\sigma^{i}\omega)\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\tilde{g}_{N,n}^{(3)}(\sigma^{i}\omega)\to\int_{\Omega}\tilde{g}_{n}^{(3)}(\omega)\ dm(\omega)=C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}C_{3}\alpha(n)\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega)

as
N\to\infty
for each
n
and a.e.
\omega
.

Step 6: Finishing up.

Recall from (
5.3
) that

(\ref{evtexpression})=\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}\left(\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\mathbbold{1})+\nu_{\sigma^{N}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}(\mathbbold{1})\right)\right).

Using (
(C4’)
) and Lemma
5.4
we see that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\mathbbold{1})+\nu_{\sigma^{N}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}(\mathbbold{1})\right)\right)=1
for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.
By (
5.29
) and Steps 3, 4, and 5 we see that for any
n

(5.35)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\left(g^{(1)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)+g^{(2)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)+g^{(3)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)\right)\right)\exp\left(-\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{2}}{2(1-y)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\left(g^{(1)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)+g^{(2)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)-\tilde{g}^{(3)}_{N,n}(\sigma^{i}\omega)\right)\right)\exp\left(-\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{2}}{2(1-y)^{2}}\right),

where
0\leq y\leq Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}
.
We now treat the Taylor remainder terms.
From Steps 3, 4, and 5 for all
N
sufficiently large we have and for almost all
\omega
:

(5.36)

\displaystyle|g^{(1)}_{N,n}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon_{N}}(|t|_{\infty}+W),

(5.37)

\displaystyle|g^{(2)}_{N,n}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{C_{\varepsilon_{N}}C_{2}C_{3}(C_{2}C_{\varepsilon_{N}}+C_{2}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha)}\cdot\frac{C_{3}n(|t|_{\infty}+W)^{2}}{N},

(5.38)

\displaystyle|g^{(3)}_{N,n}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{g}^{(3)}_{N,n}:=C_{\varphi_{0}}C_{2}C_{3}\alpha(|t|_{\infty}+W).

Further,

(5.39)

\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{2}}{2(1-y)^{2}}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}^{2}}{2(1-Y_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n})^{2}}=\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\frac{(G_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}/N)^{2}}{2(1-(G_{\sigma^{i}\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}/N))^{2}},

where
G_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N},n}:=g^{(1)}_{N,n}+g^{(2)}_{N,n}+g^{(3)}_{N,n}
.
Using the bounds (
5.36
)–(
5.38
) we see that (
5.39
) approaches
0
for almost all
\omega
for each
n
as
N\to\infty
.
Therefore, combining the expressions developed in Steps 3, 4, and 5 for the
N\to\infty
limits with (
5.35
) we have

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0,n}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega)\right)\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}\leq\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0,n}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega)+C_{2}C_{3}C_{\varphi_{0}}\alpha(n)\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega)\right).

Recalling the definitions of
\theta_{\omega,0,n}
(
5.31
) and
\theta_{\omega,0}
(
2.20
) and the fact that
0\leq\theta_{\omega,0,n}\leq 1
(by (
4.11
)), we may use dominated convergence to take the
n\to\infty
limit to obtain

\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega)\right)\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}\leq\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}\theta_{\omega,0}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega)\right),

thus completing the proof that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\nu_{\omega,0}\left(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}=\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\theta_{\omega,0}\,dm(\omega)\right).

To see that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu_{\omega,0}\left(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)
is also equal to this value, we simply recall that (
5.4
) gives that

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega,0}(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}})=\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}\left(\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})+\nu_{\sigma^{N}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right)\right).

Now since (
(C4’)
) and Lemma
5.4
together give that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}(\varphi_{\omega,0})+\nu_{\sigma^{N}\omega,0}\left(Q_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}(\varphi_{\omega,0})\right)\right)=1

for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
, we must in fact have that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu_{\omega,0}\left(X_{\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}\right)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{N}}{\lambda_{\omega,0}^{N}}=\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\theta_{\omega,0}\,dm(\omega)\right),

which completes the proof of Theorem
5.5
.

∎

5.2.
The relationship between condition (
1.5
) and the Hüsler condition

We now return to the discussion initiated in the Introduction to compare our assumption (
1.5
) for the thresholds
z_{\omega,N}
with the Hüsler type condition (
1.4
).
We show that in the more general situation considered in our paper with random boundary level
t_{\omega}
, the limit (
1.4
) will follow from the simpler assumption (
1.5
), provided we replace
t
in (
1.4
) with the expectation of
t_{\omega}.

Recall our assumption for the choice of the thresholds (
(S)
) is
\mu_{\omega,0}(h_{\omega}(x)>z_{\omega,N})=(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})/N
,
where
\xi_{\omega,N}
goes to zero almost surely when
N\rightarrow\infty
and
\xi_{\omega,N}\leq W
for a.e.
\omega
. It is immediate to see by dominated convergence that:

(5.40)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\Omega}|N\mu_{\omega,0}(h_{\omega}(x)>z_{\omega,N})-t_{\omega}|\ dm(\omega)=0.

Applying our non-standard ergodic Lemma
5.6
with
g_{N}(\omega):=N\mu_{\omega,0}(h_{\omega}(x)>z_{\omega,N})
and
g(\omega):=t_{\omega}
,
one may transform the sum in (
1.4
) as follows:

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}N\mu_{\omega,0}(h_{\sigma^{j}\omega}(T^{j}_{\omega}(x))>z_{\sigma^{j}\omega,N})=\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\ dm(\omega),

which is the condition (
1.4
) with
t
replaced by the expectation of
t_{\omega}.

5.3.
Hitting time statistics

It is well known that in the deterministic setting there is a close relationship between extreme value theory and the statistics of first hitting time, see for instance
[
25
,
41
]
. We now show how our Theorem
5.5
, with a slight modification, can be interpreted in terms of a suitable definition of (quenched) first hitting time distribution.
Let us consider as in the previous sections, a sequence of small random holes
\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}:=\{H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\}_{j\geq 0},
and define
the first random hitting time as

\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}(x)=\inf\{k\geq 1,T^{k}_{\omega}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{k}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}\}.

We recall that the usual statistics of hitting times is written in the form

\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}>t\right),

for nonnegative values of
t.

Since the sets
H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}
have measure tending to zero when
N\rightarrow\infty,
and therefore the first hitting times could eventually grow to infinity, one needs a rescaling in order to get a meaningful limit distribution. This is achieved in the next Proposition.
In our current setting, condition (
(S)
) reads:

\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})=\frac{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N}}{N},

with

\lim_{N\to\infty}\xi_{\omega,N}=0
for a.e.
\omega
and

|\xi_{\omega,N}|\leq W
for a.e.
\omega
and all
N\geq 1
.

Proposition 5.7
.

If our random open system
(\Omega,\allowbreak m,\allowbreak\sigma,\allowbreak\mathcal{J}_{0},\allowbreak T,\allowbreak\mathcal{B},\allowbreak\mathcal{L}_{0},\allowbreak\nu_{0},\allowbreak\varphi_{0},\allowbreak H_{\varepsilon})
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
5.5
with the sequence
H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}
verifying condition (
(S)
),
then the first random hitting time satisfies the limit, for
\omega

m
-a.e.

(5.41)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}\right)=\exp\left(-{\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\theta_{\omega,0}dm}\right).

Proof.

For
N\geq 1
the event,

(5.42)

\{\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}>N\}=\{x\in I;T_{\omega}(x)\in H_{\sigma\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c},\dots,T^{N}_{\omega}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{N}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c}\}

is also equal to

T^{-1}_{\omega}\left(x\in I;x\in H_{\sigma\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c},T_{\sigma\omega}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{2}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c},\dots,T_{\sigma\omega}^{N-1}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{N}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c}\right).

Then, by equivariance of
\mu_{0}
we obtain the link between the statistics of hitting time and extreme value theory:

(5.43)

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}>N\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega,0}\left(T^{-1}_{\omega}\left(x\in I:x\in H_{\sigma\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c},T_{\sigma\omega}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{2}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c},\dots,T_{\sigma\omega}^{N-1}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{N}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{\sigma\omega,0}\left(x\in H_{\sigma\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c},T_{\sigma\omega}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{2}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c},\dots,T_{\sigma\omega}^{N-1}(x)\in H_{\sigma^{N}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}}^{c}\right)

(5.44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{\sigma\omega,0}(X_{\sigma\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}}).

In order to rescale the eventually growing first random hitting times, we invoke the condition (
(S)
); by substituting
N=(t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N})/\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})
in the LHS of (
5.43
)
we have

(5.45)

\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}>N\right)=\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N}\right).

Our final preparation before applying Theorem
5.5
is to show that

(5.46)

|\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N}\right)-\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}\right)|\rightarrow 0,\ N\rightarrow\infty.

Since by a standard trick, see for instance eq. 5.3.6 in
[
41
]
,

\{\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}\}\backslash\{\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N}\}\subset\bigcup_{j=\left\lceil{\frac{t_{\omega}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}}\right\rceil}^{\left\lceil{\frac{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,n}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}}\right\rceil}T_{\omega}^{-j}(H_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})

we have by equivariance

|\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,N}\right)-\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}\right)|\leq\sum_{j=\left\lceil{\frac{t_{\omega}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}}\right\rceil}^{\left\lceil{\frac{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,n}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}}\right\rceil}\mu_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}({H}_{\sigma^{j}\omega,N}).

For
N
large enough:

\sum_{j=\left\lceil{\frac{t_{\omega}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}}\right\rceil}^{\left\lceil{\frac{t_{\omega}+\xi_{\omega,n}}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}}\right\rceil}\mu_{\sigma^{j}\omega,0}({H}_{\sigma^{j}\omega,N})\leq\left\lceil{\frac{|\xi_{\omega,N}|}{\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})}}\right\rceil\frac{|t|_{\infty}+W}{N}\leq\left\lceil{\frac{|\xi_{\omega,N}|\ N}{t_{\omega}-|\xi_{\omega,N}|}}\right\rceil\frac{|t|_{\infty}+W}{N},

which goes to zero by (
(S)
).
Recalling
t_{\omega}>0
for a.e.
\omega
, the final expression above goes to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
for
\omega

m
-a.e.

Using (
5.44
)–(
5.46
) and noting that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu_{\sigma\omega,0}(X_{\sigma\omega,N-1,\varepsilon_{N}})
is nonrandom, applying Theorem
5.5
yields

(5.47)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mu_{\omega,0}\left(\tau_{\omega,\mathcal{H}_{\omega,N}}\mu_{\omega,0}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon_{N}})>t_{\omega}\right)=\exp\left(-\int_{\Omega}t_{\omega}\theta_{\omega,0}\ dm\right).

∎

6.
Quenched Thermodynamic Formalism for Random Open Interval Maps via Perturbation

In this section we present an explicit class of random piecewise-monotonic interval maps for which our Theorem
4.6
, Corollary
4.9
, and Theorem
5.5
apply.
Using a perturbative approach, we introduce a family of small random holes parameterised by
\varepsilon>0
into a random closed dynamical system, and for every small
\varepsilon
we prove (i) the existence of a unique random conformal measure
\left\{\nu_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
with fiberwise support in
X_{\omega,\infty,\varepsilon}
and (ii) a unique random absolutely continuous invariant measure
\left\{\mu_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
which satisfies an exponential decay of correlations and is the unique relative equilibirum state for the random open system
(\Omega,\allowbreak m,\allowbreak\sigma,\allowbreak\mathcal{J}_{0},\allowbreak T,\allowbreak\mathcal{B},\allowbreak\mathcal{L}_{0},\allowbreak\nu_{0},\allowbreak\varphi_{0},\allowbreak H_{\varepsilon})
. In addition, we prove the existence of a random absolutely continuous (with respect to
\nu_{\omega,0}
) conditionally invariant probability measure
\left\{\varrho_{\omega,\varepsilon}\right\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
with fiberwise support in
[0,1]\backslash H_{\omega,\varepsilon}
.

We now suppose that the spaces
\mathcal{J}_{\omega,0}=[0,1]
for each
\omega\in\Omega
and the maps
T_{\omega}:[0,1]\to[0,1]
are surjective, finitely-branched, piecewise monotone, nonsingular (with respect to Lebesgue), and that there exists
C\geq 1
such that

(E1)

\displaystyle\esssup_{\omega}|T_{\omega}^{\prime}|\leq C\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\esssup_{\omega}D(T_{\omega})\leq C,

where
D(T_{\omega}):=\sup_{y\in[0,1]}\#T_{\omega}^{-1}(y)
.
We let
\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,0}
denote the (finite) monotonicity partition of
T_{\omega}
and for each
n\geq 2
we let
\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,0}^{(n)}
denote the partition of monotonicity of
T_{\omega}^{n}
.

(MC)

The map
\sigma:\Omega\to\Omega
is a homeomorphism, the skew-product map
T:\Omega\times[0,1]\to\Omega\times[0,1]
is measurable, and
\omega\mapsto T_{\omega}
has countable range.

Remark 6.1
.

Under assumption (
(MC)
), the family of transfer operator cocycles
\{\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\geq 0}
satisfies the conditions of Theorem 17
[
29
]
(
m
-continuity and
\sigma
a homeomorphism). Note that condition (
(MC)
)
implies that
T
satisfies (
(M1)
) and the cocycle generated by
\mathcal{L}_{0}
satisfies condition (
(M2)
).

Let the
variation
of
f:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
on
Z\subset[0,1]
be

\displaystyle\text{{\rm var}}_{Z}(f)=\underset{x_{0}<\dots<x_{k},\,x_{j}\in Z}{\sup}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\left\lvert f(x_{j+1})-f(x_{j})\right\rvert,

and
\text{{\rm var}}(f):=\text{{\rm var}}_{[0,1]}(f)
. We let
\text{{\rm BV}}=\text{{\rm BV}}([0,1])
denote the set of functions on
[0,1]
that have bounded variation.
Given a non-atomic and fully supported measure
\nu
(i.e. for any nondegenerate interval
J\subseteq[0,1]
we have
\nu(J)>0
) we let
\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}\subseteq L^{\infty}(\nu)
be the set of (equivalence classes of) functions of
bounded variation
on
[0,1]
, with norm given by

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}}:=\underset{\tilde{f}=f\ \nu\text{ a.e.}}{\inf}\text{{\rm var}}(\tilde{f})+\nu(|f|).

If we require to emphasise that elements of
\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}
are equivalence classes, we denote these by
[f]_{\nu}
(resp.
[f]_{1}
).
Note that if
f\in\text{{\rm BV}}
is a function of bounded variation, then it is always possible to choose a representative of minimal variation from the equivalence class
[f]_{\nu}
.
We define
\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}\subseteq L^{\infty}(\text{{\rm Leb}})
and
\|\makebox[4.30554pt]{{$\cdot$}}\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}}
similarly, with the measure
\nu
replaced with Lebesgue measure. We denote the supremum norm on
L^{\infty}(\text{{\rm Leb}})
by
\|\makebox[4.30554pt]{{$\cdot$}}\|_{\infty,1}
.
It follows from Rychlik
[
46
]
that
\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}
and
\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}
are Banach spaces.
The following proposition gives the equivalence of the norms
\|\makebox[4.30554pt]{{$\cdot$}}\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}}
and
\|\makebox[4.30554pt]{{$\cdot$}}\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Given a fully supported and non-atomic measure
\nu
on
[0,1]
and
f\in\text{{\rm BV}}
we have that

(1/2)\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}}\leq\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}}\leq 2\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}}.

Proof.

We first show that for
f\in\text{{\rm BV}}
we have

(6.1)

\displaystyle[f]_{\nu}\cap\text{{\rm BV}}=[f]_{1}\cap\text{{\rm BV}}.

To see this let
\tilde{f}\in[f]_{\nu}\cap\text{{\rm BV}}
. As
\nu
is a fully supported and non-atomic measure, we must have that the set
\{x:f(x)\neq\tilde{f}(x)\}
is countable. Thus
\tilde{f}\in[f]_{1}\cap\text{{\rm BV}}
. As
Leb
is also fully supported and non-atomic the same reasoning implies that the reverse inclusion also holds, proving (
6.1
). As a direct consequence of (
6.1
) we have that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\underset{\tilde{f}=f\ \nu\text{ a.e.}}{\inf}\text{{\rm var}}(\tilde{f})=\underset{\tilde{f}=f\ \text{{\rm Leb}}\text{ a.e.}}{\inf}\text{{\rm var}}(\tilde{f}).

Since
f
is continuous everywhere except on a set of at most countably many points, letting
\mathcal{C}
denote the set of intervals of continuity for
f
, we have

(6.3)

\displaystyle\Lebessinf_{[0,1]}f

\displaystyle=\inf_{J\in\mathcal{C}}\Lebessinf_{J}f=\inf_{J\in\mathcal{C}}\inf_{J}f=\inf_{J\in\mathcal{C}}\nuessinf_{J}f=\nuessinf_{[0,1]}f.

Using similar reasoning we must also have

(6.4)

\displaystyle\Lebesssup f=\nuesssup f.

Combining (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}}=\inf_{\tilde{f}=f\ \text{{\rm Leb}}\ a.e.}\text{{\rm var}}(\tilde{f})+\text{{\rm Leb}}(|f|)

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf_{\tilde{f}=f\ \text{{\rm Leb}}\ a.e.}\text{{\rm var}}(\tilde{f})+\Lebessinf|f|

\displaystyle=2\inf_{\tilde{f}=f\ \nu\ a.e.}\text{{\rm var}}(\tilde{f})+\nuessinf|f|

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf_{\tilde{f}=f\ \nu\ a.e.}\text{{\rm var}}(\tilde{f})+\nu(|f|)\leq 2\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}}.

Similarly, using (
6.2
) and (
6.4
) we have
\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu}}\leq 2\|f\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}}
, and thus the proof is complete.

∎

Proposition
6.2
will be used later to provide (non-random) equivalence of
\|\makebox[4.30554pt]{{$\cdot$}}\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu_{\omega,0}}}
and
\|\makebox[4.30554pt]{{$\cdot$}}\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}_{1}}
for each
\omega\in\Omega
.
We set
J_{\omega}:=|T_{\omega}^{\prime}|
and define the random Perron–Frobenius operator, acting on functions in
BV

P_{\omega}(f)(x):=\sum_{y\in T_{\omega}^{-1}(x)}\frac{f(y)}{J_{\omega}(y)}.

The operator
P
satisfies the well-known property that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]}P_{\omega}(f)\,d\text{{\rm Leb}}=\int_{[0,1]}f\,d\text{{\rm Leb}}

for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
and all
f\in\text{{\rm BV}}
.
Recall from Section
3
that
g_{0}=\left\{g_{\omega,0}\right\}_{\omega\in\Omega}
and that

\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}(f)(x):=\sum_{y\in T_{\omega}^{-1}(x)}g_{\omega,0}(y)f(y),\quad f\in\text{{\rm BV}}.

We assume that the weight function
g_{\omega,0}
lies in
BV
for each
\omega\in\Omega
and satisfies

(E2)

\esssup_{\omega}\|g_{\omega,0}\|_{\infty,1}<\infty,

and

(E3)

\essinf_{\omega}\inf g_{\omega,0}>0.

Note that (
E1
) and (
E2
) together imply

(6.6)

\displaystyle\esssup_{\omega}\|\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}\mathbbold{1}\|_{\infty,1}\leq\esssup_{\omega}D(T_{\omega})\|g_{\omega,0}\|_{\infty,1}<\infty

and

(6.7)

\displaystyle\esssup_{\omega}\|g_{\omega,0}J_{\omega}\|_{\infty,1}<\infty.

We also assume a uniform covering condition
2
2

2

We could replace the covering condition with the assumption of a strongly contracting potential. See
[
3
]
for details.

:

(E4)

For every subinterval
J\subset[0,1]
there is a
k=k(J)
such that for a.e.
\omega
one has
T^{k}_{\omega}(J)=[0,1]
.

Concerning the open system we assume that the holes
H_{\omega,\varepsilon}\subseteq[0,1]
are chosen so that assumption
(
(A)
) holds. We also assume for each
\omega\in\Omega
and each
\varepsilon>0
that
H_{\omega,\varepsilon}
is composed of a finite union of intervals such that

(E5)

There is a uniform-in-
\varepsilon
and uniform-in-
\omega
upper bound on the number of connected components of
H_{\omega,\varepsilon}
,

and

(E6)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\esssup_{\omega}\text{{\rm Leb}}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon})=0,

and

(EX)

There exists an
\varepsilon>0
and an open neighborhood
\tilde{H}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\supseteq H_{\omega,\varepsilon}
such that

T_{\omega}(U_{\omega})\supseteq\tilde{H}_{\sigma\omega,\varepsilon}^{c}
, where
U_{\omega}:=\cup_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,0}}\overline{Z}_{\varepsilon}
and
\overline{Z_{\varepsilon}}
denotes the closure of
Z_{\varepsilon}\in A_{\omega,\varepsilon}:=\{Z\cap\tilde{H}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{c}:Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,0}\}
with
m(\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\#A_{\omega,\varepsilon}\geq 2\right\})>0
.

Remark 6.3
.

Assumption (
(EX)
) is satisfied for any random open system such that each map contains at least two intervals of monotonicity, the holes are contained in the interior of exactly one interval of monotonicity, and the image of the complement of the hole is the full interval, i.e.
T_{\omega}(H_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{c})=[0,1]
. In particular, (
(EX)
) is satisfied if there exists a full branch outside of the hole.

The following proposition ensures that condition (
(X)
) holds.

Proposition 6.4
.

The assumption (
(EX)
) implies (
(X)
).

Proof.

In light of Remark
3.5
, to show that (
(X)
) is satisfied, it suffices to show that there is some
\varepsilon>0
such that
X_{\omega,\infty,\varepsilon}\neq\emptyset
for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.

Let
\tilde{T}_{\omega,Z}
denote the continuous extension of
T_{\omega}
onto
\overline{Z}_{\varepsilon}
for each
Z_{\varepsilon}\in A_{\omega,\varepsilon}
, and let
\tilde{X}_{\omega,\infty,\varepsilon}
denote the survivor set for the open system consisting of the maps
\tilde{T}_{\omega}
and holes
\tilde{H}_{\omega,\varepsilon}
. By Proposition
3.6
(taking
V_{\omega}=[0,1]\backslash\tilde{H}_{\omega,\varepsilon}
and
U_{\omega,j}=\overline{Z}_{\varepsilon}
for each
1\leq j\leq\#A_{\omega,\varepsilon}
) we see that
\tilde{X}_{\omega,\infty,\varepsilon}
is uncountable.
Let

\mathcal{D}_{\omega}:=\bigcup_{j\geq 0}\tilde{T}_{\omega}^{-j}(\cup_{Z_{\varepsilon}\in A_{\sigma^{j}\omega,\varepsilon}}\overline{Z}_{\varepsilon}\backslash Z_{\varepsilon}).

Since the survivor set for the original (unmodified) open system

X_{\omega,\infty,\varepsilon}\subseteq\tilde{X}_{\omega,\infty,\varepsilon}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{\omega}
,
and since
\mathcal{D}_{\omega}
is at most countable, we must in fact have that
X_{\omega,\infty,\varepsilon}\neq\emptyset
, thus satisfying (
(X)
).
∎

Further, we suppose that for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
and all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small

(E7)

\displaystyle T_{\omega}(\mathcal{J}_{\omega,\varepsilon})=[0,1]

and there exists
n^{\prime}\geq 1
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

3
3

3

Note that the
9
appearing in (
E8
) is not optimal. See
[
4
,
2
]
for how this assumption may be improved.

(E8)

9\cdot\esssup_{\omega}\|g_{\omega,0}^{(n^{\prime})}\|_{\infty,1}<\essinf_{\omega}\inf_{0\leq\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\inf\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{n^{\prime}}\mathbbold{1},

where

\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}(f)(x):=\mathcal{L}_{\omega,0}(\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{c}}f)(x)=\sum_{y\in T_{\omega}^{-1}(x)}g_{\omega,\varepsilon}(y)f(y),\quad f\in\text{{\rm BV}}

and
g_{\omega,\varepsilon}:=g_{\omega,0}\mathbbold{1}_{H_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{c}}
as in Section
3.1
.

Note that (
E7
) and (
E3
) together imply that
\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\mathbbold{1}(x)>0
for all
x\in[0,1]
:

(6.8)

\displaystyle\essinf_{\omega}\inf_{\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\inf\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}\mathbbold{1}>\essinf_{\omega}\inf g_{\omega,0}>0,

and since
\|g_{\omega,\varepsilon}\|_{\infty,1}\leq\|g_{\omega,0}\|_{\infty,1}
for all
\varepsilon>0
, (
E8
) is equivalent to the following

(6.9)

\displaystyle 9\cdot\sup_{0\leq\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\esssup_{\omega}\|g_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(n^{\prime})}\|_{\infty,1}<\essinf_{\omega}\inf_{0\leq\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\inf\mathcal{L}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{n^{\prime}}\mathbbold{1}.

Remark 6.5
.

Note that the assumption (
E7
) is equivalent to there existing

N^{\prime}\geq 1
such that for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
and all
\varepsilon\geq 0
sufficiently small

T_{\omega}^{N^{\prime}}(X_{\omega,N^{\prime}-1,\varepsilon})=[0,1].

Indeed, since the surviving sets are forward invariant (
3.4
), we have that

T_{\omega}^{N^{\prime}-1}(X_{\omega,N^{\prime}-1,\varepsilon})\subseteq X_{\sigma^{N^{\prime}-1}\omega,0,\varepsilon}=\mathcal{J}_{\sigma^{N^{\prime}-1}\omega,\varepsilon}
, and thus,

[0,1]=T_{\omega}^{N^{\prime}}(X_{\omega,N^{\prime}-1,\varepsilon})\supseteq T_{\sigma^{N^{\prime}-1}\omega}(\mathcal{J}_{\sigma^{N^{\prime}-1}\omega,\varepsilon}).

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\omega\in\Omega
we let
\mathscr{A}_{\omega,0}^{(n)}
be the collection of all finite partitions of
[0,1]
such that

(6.10)

\displaystyle\text{{\rm var}}_{A_{i}}(g_{\omega,0}^{(n)})\leq 2\|g_{\omega,0}^{(n)}\|_{\infty,1}

for each
\mathcal{A}=\left\{A_{i}\right\}\in\mathscr{A}_{\omega,0}^{(n)}
.
Given
\mathcal{A}\in\mathscr{A}_{\omega,0}^{(n)}
, let
\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(n)}(\mathcal{A})
be the coarsest partition amongst all those finer than
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,0}^{(n)}
such that all elements of
\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(n)}(\mathcal{A})
are either disjoint from
X_{\omega,n-1,\varepsilon}
or contained in
X_{\omega,n-1,\varepsilon}
.

Remark 6.6
.

Note that if
\text{{\rm var}}_{Z}(g_{\omega,0})\leq 2\|g_{\omega,0}^{(n)}\|_{\infty,1}
for each
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,0}^{(n)}
then we can take the partition
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,0}^{(n)}
. Furthermore, the
2
above can be replaced by some
\hat{}\alpha\geq 0
(depending on
g_{\omega,0}
) following the techniques of
[
4
,
2
]
.

Define the subcollection

(6.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,*,\varepsilon}^{(n)}:=\{Z\in\widehat{}\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(n)}(\mathcal{A}):Z\subseteq X_{\omega,n-1,\varepsilon}\}.

Recalling that
g_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(n)}:=g_{\omega,0}^{(n)}\mathbbold{1}_{X_{\omega,n-1,\varepsilon}}
, (
6.10
) implies that

(6.12)

\displaystyle\text{{\rm var}}_{Z}(g_{\omega,\varepsilon}^{(n)})\leq 2\|g_{\omega,0}^{(n)}\|_{\infty,1}

for each
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,*,\varepsilon}^{(n)}
.
We assume the following covering condition for the open system

(E9)

There exists
k_{o}(n^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}
such that for
m
-a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
, all
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, and all
Z\in\mathcal{Z}_{\omega,*,\varepsilon}^{(n^{\prime})}
we have
T_{\omega}^{k_{o}(n^{\prime})}(Z)=[0,1]
, where
n^{\prime}
is the number coming from (
E8
).

Remark 6.7
.

Note that the uniform open covering time assumption (
(E9)
) clearly holds if (
(E4)
) holds and if there are only finitely many maps
T_{\omega}
. In Remark
C.2
we present an alternative assumption to (
(E9)
).

The following lemma extends several results in
[
22
]
from the specific weight
g_{\omega,0}=1/|T^{\prime}_{\omega}|
to general weights satisfying the conditions just outlined.

Lemma 6.8
.

Assume that a family of random piecewise-monotonic interval maps
\{T_{\omega}\}
satisfies (
E2
), (
E3
), and (
(E4)
), as well as (
E8
) and (
(MC)
) for
\varepsilon=0
.
Then
(
(C1’)
) and the
\varepsilon=0
parts of (
(C2)
), (
(C3)
), (
(C4’)
), (
(C5’)
), and (
(C7’)
) as well as (
(CCM)
) hold.
Further,
\nu_{\omega,0}
is fully supported, condition (
(C4’)
) holds with
C_{f}=K
, for some
K<\infty
, and with
\alpha(N)=\gamma^{N}
for some
\gamma<1
.

Proof.

See Appendix
A
.
∎

In what follows we consider transfer operators acting on the Banach spaces
\mathcal{B}_{\omega}=\text{{\rm BV}}_{\nu_{\omega,0}}
for a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
.
The norm we will use is
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{B}_{\omega}}=\|\cdot\|_{\text{{\rm BV}}}:=\text{{\rm var}}(\cdot)+\nu_{\omega,0}(|\cdot|)
.
As
